# Manual Tasks

Single global backlog of items only Darcy can resolve (production verification, browser-only tests, deletes, decisions, credential steps, restarts).

**Append-only with monotonic IDs.** Sessions add new tasks at the bottom and close existing ones in place. Never renumber.

## Status legend

- `open`     — waiting for Darcy
- `done`     — completed (note the closing session ID)
- `wontfix`  — superseded, no longer relevant, or rejected
- `blocked`  — waiting on something other than Darcy (note what)

## How sessions update this file

When closing a session:

1. **Closing existing tasks**: find each `MT-NNNN` you resolved, change `open` → `done`, append `(closed by <session-id>)` to the line.
2. **Adding new tasks**: append at the bottom under `## Open tasks`. Look at the last 10 open tasks first to avoid dupes — if your task already exists, reference its existing ID in your session record instead of adding a new one.
3. **ID generation**: take the highest existing `MT-NNNN`, add 1. Pad to 3 digits.

- **MT-266** [open] HIGH / SAFETY — Adolescent antihero self-harm-adjacent framing ⚠️ (created by ux6b) — The "double life" content is mostly well-handled (the *"where do you draw the line"* moral-code prompt at `superhero_costume_screen.dart:660-666` is the safety valve — KEEP it). The original flag: power **"Ghost — easy to vanish for real"** (`superhero_power_screen.dart:240`) + secret chip **"That I'm not okay"** (`superhero_costume_screen.dart:646`) together read as romanticizing disappearance; and the Identity page is concealment-only. **UPDATE (ux6b, 2026-06-15) — partial fix shipped:** the client "vanish for real" string is FIXED — `superhero_power_screen.dart:240` now reads *"Move unseen and unheard — the better you hide, the more alone you are."*, aligned to the backend's already-safe framing (`superhero_matrix.py:1084`, where the cost is **loneliness**, not disappearance). **(a) DONE — TWO complementary fixes, reconciled & merged 2026-06-20 (a784):** (1) ux6b's `ff751112` **secret-care mandate** in `_build_superhero_prompt_adolescent` (fires whenever `hero_secret` is set) — *"if the secret is about their own wellbeing/struggle, the chapter must move them at least one step toward being SEEN by someone who responds with care (not pity, not fixing), and the AFTERMATH must leave a thread of connection or hope; distress is never aesthetic, isolation is never the resolution"* (live-verified n=1 via gpt-5-mini: arc "guarded → partially seen", ended on a friend's "You okay?" framed as "an opening"); (2) #292 **softened the `secret_bullet`** from *"let the story press on it"* → *"let the pressure crack toward being known, never toward deeper hiding"* (applies to ALL secrets, both the shared `_antihero_brief` and the single-shot builder). Both now co-exist on the premise line (`{seen_by_bullet}{secret_care_mandate}`). **(b) DONE via #292:** new nullable `heroSeenBy`/`hero_seen_by` field threaded through all 8 layers (wizard_data → costume Identity page 4th section "Who gets to see the real you?" + Surprise-me seed → mapper → api_service_manager → magic_review read-out → story_routes → story_tasks → prompt_service); Adolescent-only, optional, blank = unchanged behavior; the new `seen_by_bullet` gives the chapter a concrete person to move *toward* ("a step toward being known — earned, not tidy"), with a generic connection anchor even when blank. Tests: +4 backend (`test_superhero_prompt_adolescent.py`, incl. the "That I'm not okay" safety assertion) + 6 Dart (`wizard_data_superhero_test.dart`); full antihero suite green, `flutter analyze` clean. **Remaining = (c) only:** route the whole antihero tone past a second (ideally clinical) reviewer before launch — the wording + n=1 test do not substitute for human sign-off. **Watch-item for that reviewer (ux6b, 2026-06-20 live gen):** in a 2nd `gpt-5-mini` sample (secret="That I'm not okay", seen_by="one friend who's earned it") the model produced the intended being-seen arc cleanly ("the possibility of being seen without being fixed… It wasn't saving. Reveal lives at the *edge* of the story (never inside it), reusing the `_triggerData` label map (`'a limit is set'`→"Hearing no") + the **Saga Record** separate-reflection-screen pattern. Plus one parent toggle in the Big Feelings panel. **Forks RESOLVED (2026-06-14, Darcy):** (1) reveal = **Pull** — one quiet "Why this story? 💛" button at story end, all bands, never inside the story; PLUS a **guaranteed parent-side** "what each story practiced" view in Parent Controls so disclosure to the trusted adult always happens even if the child never taps. (2) precision = **per-story** — backend echoes a `practiced` field naming the exact focus this story used. (3) Sprout 3-5 = **relational-only** ("a grown-up who loves you picked this for you 💛", no skill name/lesson framing); Explorer 6-8 gentle skill name + co-read Q; Adventurer 9-12 direct + agency-preserving; Adolescent 13-17 full transparency. **BUILD CHECKLIST:** (a) backend `practiced` field on story-gen response (which `ParentHiddenContext` focus was woven in); (b) child-side "Why this story? 💛" button → Story Notes reveal screen (reuse Saga Record screen pattern + `_triggerData` label map); (c) parent-side per-story "what each story practiced" view in `parent_controls_screen.dart`; (d) **addendum to HIDDEN_PARENT_LAYER_SPEC.md** reversing `child_visibility: hidden` for older bands (values change, not a bug) + fix the Big Feelings privacy-note copy which currently promises "Your child will never see these choices". See memory [[boundary_skills_feature]]. Design effort: medium; values-aligned, not urgent. **BUILD COMPLETE (9128, 2026-06-15) → PR #279** (branch `worktree-story-notes-mt254`, 8 commits): all checklist items (a)–(d) shipped, plus extras — disclose ALL configured focuses (not just primary; named naturally up to 3); persist `practiced` on `StoryLocal` so re-opened guided stories keep the disclosure; correct band on reopen via a `practicedAge` param (the global theme would otherwise disclose at the active profile's band). New files: `lib/models/story_notes.dart`, `lib/screens/story_notes_screen.dart`. 20 Dart + 87 backend tests green, `flutter analyze` clean. Multi-character/hero-creator paths correctly show no button (not Big Feelings). Awaiting owner review/merge + device-verify → **MT-260**. **MERGED & CLOSED (2026-06-15):** #279 squash-merged to `main` (merge commit `085b7924`), all CI green, auto-deployed to prod. Two follow-ups carried to MT-260: (1) on-device per-band verification of the disclosure; (2) the in-code TODO to unify the `_focusCopy` keys in `story_notes.dart` with the `_triggerData` label map so the labels can't drift.

The runner's SwiftPM artifact cache is poisoned (a partial/duplicate Sentry zip), and a plain re-run does NOT clear it. **Not a code bug** — iOS built green on #243/#249 and on #252's first run before a Python-only black-format commit; nothing in the change touches iOS. iOS build is **not a required check** (no branch protection contexts), so it blocked nothing — #252 merged fine — but `main` will show a red iOS check until a fresh runner/cache. **Action (owner / when convenient):** if it persists on the next PR, clear the GitHub Actions caches for the iOS workflow (Actions → Caches → delete SwiftPM/`org.swift.swiftpm` entries) or add a `rm -rf ~/Library/Caches/org.swift.swiftpm` step before `flutter build ios` in the iOS job, or pin/skip SwiftPM (`flutter config --no-enable-swift-package-manager` in CI) per the Flutter SPM-opt-out doc. ~15 min. Numbered 239 (not 236) to avoid colliding with MT-236/237/238 just created on `main` via #242. **(closed by caba — fixed at the source via PR #254: added a "Clear poisoned SwiftPM artifact cache" step (`rm -rf ~/Library/Caches/org.swift.swiftpm` + DerivedData SourcePackages + `ios/.build`) before the build in BOTH `ios-build.yml` and `ios-testflight.yml`. The iOS build went green on #254 and again on the full #257 run — no longer flaky.)**
- **MT-238** [open] LAUNCH-GATE — `ENCRYPTION_KEY` not set in production; server-side BYOK key-save errors (found 2026-06-07 during continuity setup) — The Railway `story-weaver-app` service has **no** `ENCRYPTION_KEY` variable (verified in the Variables list — alphabetically absent between `ELEVENLABS_API_KEY` and `ENV`). `backend/encryption_utils.py:34` raises `ValueError("ENCRYPTION_KEY environment variable not set")` whenever `encrypt_api_key`/`decrypt_api_key` runs, and that fires when a user stores their BYOK Gemini key server-side (`backend/routes/api_key_routes.py:85` → `user.gemini_api_key_encrypted`). So **server-side BYOK key-save 500s in prod today** — latent only because there are no real users pre-launch. **Fix:** generate a 32-byte hex key (`python -c "import secrets; print(secrets.token_hex(32))"`), set `ENCRYPTION_KEY` in Railway, and back it up to the continuity vault the instant it's set (once keys are stored with it, losing it makes them unrecoverable). Do before any BYOK server-storage goes live. ~10 min. **Corrects** the 13-continuity audit + RECOVERY.md, which assumed the key already existed in Railway — it does not.

- **MT-190** [open] Wire 3 Sentry issue-alert rules for the reliability signals emitted by the FMEA Phase 4 work (created by 83c8) — The signals exist in code (`backend/tasks/monitoring_tasks.py::_alert` and `backend/tasks/story_tasks.py` static-fallback block, both via `sentry_sdk.capture_message` with tag `reliability_signal=<name>`). Each becomes a Sentry Issue keyed by message. In Sentry → Alerts → Create Alert → Issue Alert (environment: production), filter by tag `reliability_signal equals <value>`. Three rules: (a) name "AI providers all down", value `static_fallback`, condition "seen ≥ 3 times in 1 hour" (single occurrence can be a noisy retry; spike is the real signal); (b) name "Story queue backing up", value `celery_queue_depth_high`, condition "first seen" (the monitor only emits when depth ≥ 20); (c) name "Retention purge stopped", value `retention_purge_heartbeat_stale`, condition "first seen" (monitor re-checks every 10 min — use "first seen" to avoid spam). Then action: notify your usual channel (email or the Slack/Discord webhook from `docs/RAILWAY_ALERTS_SETUP.md`). Verify with `firstSeen:-7d` Sentry query on the tag.

- **MT-130** [done — fixed by Antigravity in commit `9375e69c` on reliability-hardening. Web-only JPEG compression of illustrations before base64 + localStorage persist; adds `image` package dep in pubspec.yaml. Keeps the saved-stories blob well under the ~5 MB SharedPreferences/localStorage ceiling. Native (Isar) path unchanged. (closed by dade)] Web illustration-persistence storage ceiling (created by b667) — Illustration persistence (commit `bebe13f5`) base64-encodes the cover + per-page images into `StoryLocal`. On native (Isar) this is fine. On **web** the entire saved-stories list is one SharedPreferences string key (`isar_stories`); every illustrated multi-page story inflates that single blob, and SharedPreferences/localStorage has a practical ~5 MB ceiling — saving several illustrated multi-page stories could eventually hit it. **Options:** move web image storage to IndexedDB (blob store), downscale/JPEG-compress images before base64 encoding, or cap how many stories retain full art on web. Low priority until web sees real usage. Flagged by the illustration-persistence agent.
- **MT-129** [open] Generated story illustration doesn't match the created character (created by b667) — Reported during a Sprout walkthrough: a story made with character "Jayden" produced an illustration whose character does not resemble the avatar/appearance the user created. Likely the character's saved appearance (or the generated-avatar reference image) isn't being threaded into the `/generate-illustrations` prompt/payload. **Action:** trace how character appearance flows from the saved Character / GeneratedAvatar into the illustration request — start at `lib/story_result_screen.dart` `_loadCharacterDetails`, the per-page prefetcher (`lib/services/per_page_illustration_prefetcher.dart`), and the backend illustration handler. Separate, deeper investigation — not a quick fix. **UPDATE 2026-05-19 — text-fidelity fix shipped (code-complete, awaiting live verify).** Root cause was a 3-layer drop of the avatar's real appearance plus a fabricated substitute: (1) backend `avatar_generation_service.generate_custom_avatar` ran a photo-feature vision call but discarded hair_style/skin_tone from the response; (2) `hero_creator_step` saved the `GeneratedAvatar` with `attributes:{'source':'custom_photo'}`, dropping even what the backend returned; (3) `story_result_screen._characterAppearanceForBackend` ran every field through mappers that return non-null defaults (`brown` hair / `light` skin), so the backend always got a confident *wrong* description. Fix: backend now returns photo-inferred features in `attributes`; Flutter threads them through `CharacterAvatar.generationAttributes` → `GeneratedAvatar.attributes`; `_characterAppearanceForBackend` rewritten to emit only fields backed by real data (omit unknowns, no fabrication) and to fall back to live `wizardData.generatedAvatar` for unsaved characters. Non-BYOK Flux is text-to-image only so it still can't use the avatar *image* — but it's no longer fed a false description. BYOK (own Gemini key) already grounds illustrations on the avatar image via `custom_avatar_base64` (verified). - **MT-091** [done] Replace robotic flutter_tts fallback with Google Cloud TTS WaveNet (created by b7e2) (closed by 149a — solved via Microsoft Edge TTS instead of Google Cloud WaveNet: free, no API key, natural neural voices, per-word timestamps; chain is now ElevenLabs → Edge TTS → flutter_tts. Shipped to main as commits ad1a0a0e + aa2ccb4b.) — Today's chain is `ElevenLabs → flutter_tts`. flutter_tts uses the OS's built-in TTS engine, which on Chrome/Windows (Darcy's primary test environment) renders Microsoft David/Zira voices — robotic and unnerving per Darcy. iOS/Android/macOS flutter_tts is acceptable (modern Siri/Google neural voices). **Fix:** insert Google Cloud Text-to-Speech as a middle layer — WaveNet voices, free 1M chars/mo, runs on the same Google Cloud project that hosts the Gemini API. New chain: `ElevenLabs (paid, premium) → Google Cloud TTS WaveNet (free, natural) → flutter_tts (offline last-resort)`. **Steps:** (1) Enable Cloud Text-to-Speech API on existing GCP project (Console → APIs & Services → Enable, search "Text-to-Speech"); quota tracks separately from Gemini. (2) Prefer service-account credentials over reusing GEMINI_API_KEY; add `GOOGLE_CLOUD_TTS_CREDENTIALS_JSON` env var. (3) Create `backend/google_cloud_tts_service.py` modeled on `elevenlabs_tts_service.py` — same `generate_speech(text, voice_id)` signature returning bytes. Use WaveNet voices (`en-US-Wavenet-F` warm female, `en-US-Wavenet-J` male). Chunk at 5000 chars per Cloud TTS limit. (4) Update `backend/routes/tts_routes.py::synthesize`: when ElevenLabs cap-exceeded or `ELEVENLABS_API_KEY` missing, try Cloud TTS before returning 503. Only return 503 when BOTH fail. (5) Update `lib/services/app_tts_service.dart` toast copy from "Read Aloud" to "Using natural backup voice." (6) Log Cloud TTS char usage in cost_tracker (provider='google_cloud_tts', cost_usd=0 inside free tier). (7) flutter_tts stays as THIRD fallback for offline. **Verify:** free non-BYOK user exhausts ElevenLabs cap → next narration serves WaveNet (audibly natural). Disable Wi-Fi → flutter_tts kicks in. Check `audit_log` rows: provider='google_cloud_tts', cost_usd=0.0. Estimated effort: 2-4 hours.
- **MT-090** [open] Browser-verify BYOK mid-flow photo-avatar advance (created by d6a5) — Commit `5e3e3bab` in `lib/screens/wizard_steps/hero_creator_step.dart::_maybeAdvanceFromStylePage` drops the `!_shouldShowBuildHeroPage` guard on the page-1 branch. Symptom that prompted the fix (Darcy report 2026-05-11): "after I create a magical hero from my picture as a 3 year old boy, it takes me back to the screen where I choose between boy and girl instead of the scene selection screen. Then it takes me through a different screen and makes me make another hero before letting me pick a buddy." Root cause: a non-premium user on page 1 (name/gender) taps Next → avatar gallery opens → taps "Create custom avatar" → BYOK paywall → completes BYOK → `_isPremium` flips to true → CustomAvatarScreen opens via the gallery's `onCreateCustomAvatar` callback → user finishes photo → "Use this hero!" pops with avatar. At that point `_heroPage == 1` AND now `_shouldShowBuildHeroPage` is true (both `_isPremium` and `_allowPhotoAvatar` true), so the old guard `_heroPage == 1 && !_shouldShowBuildHeroPage` was false → neither branch fired → user stranded on the boy/girl picker. Layered on top of MT-074's BYOK → custom-avatar continuation fix; that fix correctly routes the user INTO the custom-avatar wizard, this one is about advancing OUT. To verify: (1) **The actual reported flow** — fresh non-premium Sprout (age 3-5) signup, name/gender page → enter name + tap Boy → tap Next → gallery opens → tap "Create a custom avatar that looks like me!" banner → upsell dialog → "Set Up Free Premium →" → BYOK wizard → enter a valid Gemini key → wizard pops → CustomAvatarScreen sproutWelcome → "Make one that looks like me!" → favoriteColor → photo → snap selfie → wait for cartoon → tap "Use this hero!" → **expected**: archetype page (4 cards: Brave Hero, Art Maker, Super Fast, Animal Friend, all gendered to Boy) with celebration sparkle + page-3 voice prompt. **Bug repros if**: you land back on the name/gender picker. (2) **Regression on the simple skip-page-2 path** — repeat with parental photo consent OFF (so `_allowPhotoAvatar` is false → `_shouldShowBuildHeroPage` stays false even after BYOK) → tap a gallery avatar (not custom) → should still advance from page 1 → page 3. (3) **Regression on the standard premium+consent path** — fresh Sprout signup with parental consent ON + BYOK already configured (so page 2 IS visible) → tap "Turn YOU into a cartoon hero!" on page 2 → CustomAvatarScreen → Use this hero! → should advance from page 2 → page 3 normally (covered by the existing `_heroPage == 2` branch, not the modified one, but verify nothing regressed). Also check the back-arrow on page 3 after the fix — `_heroPrevPage` still uses `!_shouldShowBuildHeroPage` to decide whether to skip page 2 going backward, so a BYOK-mid-flow user hitting back from page 3 will now land on the empty/redundant page 2; that's a known minor edge case worth noting but not in scope for this fix.

- **MT-088** [open] Browser-verify custom-avatar (photo→cartoon) preserves hair length & gender presentation (created by 6ce1) — Two coupled fixes in this session's commit `01859be6`: (1) the prompt in `backend/services/avatar_generation_service.py::generate_custom_avatar` was telling Gemini to use the reference image for "head shape, skin tone, and facial structure ONLY" — which made the model invent hair freely and tended to default to long flowing storybook hair regardless of source. Updated prompt now requires hair length/style/color preservation and adds an explicit Gender Presentation directive forbidding the default-to-long-flowing-hair behavior unless the photo shows long hair. (2) `gemini_image_generator.py::generate_custom_avatar` and `avatar_generation_service.py::_analyze_photo_features` were both hardcoding `mime_type="image/jpeg"` on the reference photo upload; PNG/WebP photos were being mislabelled. Symptom that prompted the fix: Darcy uploaded a photo of a 3-year-old Chinese boy with very short hair and the cartoon came out with a chin-length bob, reading as a girl. To verify: walk a fresh Sprout signup (age 3-5, gender Boy) with parental photo consent on + BYOK configured → page 2 featured "Turn YOU into a cartoon hero!" → upload a clearly-short-haired boy photo (PNG works too) → confirm cartoon output has SHORT hair, not bob/long. Then upload a long-haired girl photo with gender Girl → confirm cartoon keeps long hair. Negative case: upload a long-haired boy photo with gender Boy → cartoon should still keep the long hair (photo trumps gender stereotype). Then test PNG specifically by uploading a screenshot-cropped headshot — should round-trip without Gemini silently failing on the mime mismatch. Watch backend logs: line `Photo analysis extracted:` should report `hair_style` matching the photo (e.g. "very short black"), and the prompt sent to Gemini should include both the new "REPLICATE the hair length, style, and color" directive and "This character is a boy/girl" anchor. If hair length still drifts, the model may need explicit retake examples or a Replicate fallback for photo-anchored avatars (the current Replicate PhotoMaker fallback at `replicate_image_generator.py::generate_custom_avatar` was untouched in this session).

- **MT-154** [done] Fix interactive-story 500 — `story_segment.image_url` is `String(500)` but stores base64 data URIs (created by MT-150 verify 2026-05-18) — `POST /generate-interactive-story` on production returns HTTP 500 `psycopg2.errors.StringDataRightTruncation`: `UPDATE story_segment SET image_url=...` fails because the segment image is a `data:image/png;base64,...` URI ~1.79M chars wide while `image_url` is `db.Column(db.String(500))` (`backend/models/interactive_story.py:95` — the column comment itself says "Base64 data URI or URL"). Any time a segment image is stored inline as base64 rather than as a hosted Cloudflare R2 URL, interactive-story creation fails. Interactive Pick-a-Path stories are the free-tier headline of the M-8 re-scope, so this is high priority. **Fix:** change `image_url` to `db.Text` in the model AND add an `ALTER TABLE story_segment ALTER COLUMN image_url TYPE TEXT` to the boot migration in `backend/app.py` (the existing `pending_cols` block only ADDs missing columns, it does not widen existing ones). Also worth checking why interactive-segment images aren't routed through the R2 upload path that regular story illustrations use. ~1 hr + deploy. **CLOSED 2026-05-19 — fixed, deployed & verified.** Changed `image_url` to `db.Text` in `backend/models/interactive_story.py` and added a Postgres boot migration (`ALTER TABLE story_segment ALTER COLUMN image_url TYPE TEXT`, dialect-guarded, idempotent) to `backend/app.py`. Deployed as commit `1d51a51b`. Verified directly against the prod Postgres — `information_schema` reports `story_segment.image_url` is now `text`; `POST /generate-interactive-story` returns HTTP 200. The R2-routing aside is a misdiagnosis: the backend has no R2 upload path for illustrations; `IllustrationCache.image_data` is itself `db.Text`, so base64-in-Text is the established pattern this fix aligns with (no follow-up MT needed).
- **MT-155** [done] Custom-avatar generation times out on production — one-free-avatar feature unusable (created by MT-150 verify 2026-05-18) — Every `POST /avatar/generate-custom-avatar` on production takes ~105–112s and returns HTTP 504 `error_code: TIMEOUT`; 6/6 attempts failed, Railway deploy logs confirm `Custom avatar generation timed out` + `slow_request ... duration 108–112s status_code 504`. Generation never finishes inside the 30s `_AVATAR_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` budget, so the success path never runs, the `custom_avatars_generated` counter never increments, and a real user can never claim even their ONE free avatar (the MT-150 "magic moment"). Two distinct problems: (1) generation genuinely takes ~110s on prod — needs root-cause (slow provider, key-rotation retries on ResourceExhausted, or a fallback chain in `avatar_generation_service` / `gemini_image_generator`); app INFO logs are absent from the Railway deploy stream so add temp logging or check Sentry / cost_tracker for the provider/path used. (2) `_AVATAR_TIMEOUT_SECONDS=30` does not actually bound request latency — when `future.result(timeout=30)` raises, the enclosing `with ThreadPoolExecutor()` block's `__exit__` does `shutdown(wait=True)` and blocks until the orphaned generation thread finishes (~110s), so the client waits ~110s for a "30s timeout" response (`backend/routes/avatar_routes.py:204-238`, same pattern at 4 other call sites in the file). Blocker for MT-150 item (c). **CLOSED 2026-05-19 — fixed & deployed (commit `4dd09056`).** Problem (2) fixed: added `_run_with_timeout()` in `avatar_routes.py` that runs the work in an executor created WITHOUT the `with` context manager and shuts it down `wait=False, cancel_futures=True`, so a 30s timeout now releases the request thread immediately instead of blocking for the full generation runtime; applied to all 5 call sites. Problem (1): `generate_custom_avatar`/`generate_pet_avatar` called `models.generate_content` with no per-attempt timeout — both routed through the bounded `_generate_content_with_timeout`, and the best-effort photo-analysis vision call bounded with a 12s non-blocking timeout. **NOTE:** the ~110s timeout is NO LONGER reproducing on prod — MT-150 re-verification generated custom avatars 4× and each completed cleanly in 15–19s; the original slowness was transient (provider latency). The fix is now defensive hardening: if generation slows again the client gets a fast bounded failure instead of a 110s hang. 32 avatar tests green.

- **MT-109** [done] Swallow `OperationalError: table already exists` from gunicorn worker race in `backend/app.py` `db.create_all()` — surfaced as a side finding of MT-093 verification: every fresh deploy was logging the error, crashing one worker, gunicorn restarting, then succeeding. Now with the backend wired to Sentry, that boot-time noise was set to page Sentry on every deploy and burn the 5K/month quota with self-noise. Root cause: gunicorn forks 2 sync workers; both call `create_app() → db.create_all()` concurrently; SQLAlchemy's existence-check + CREATE TABLE isn't atomic across processes, so the loser gets `sqlite3.OperationalError`. Fix: wrap `db.create_all()` in try/except, swallow when `'already exists' in str(err)`, log a single info line, re-raise on any other error. Verified: syntax-checked via `python -c ast.parse`. Matches the existing surrounding error-handling style (`except Exception as _mig_err: logger.warning(...)` at line 519). Side-effect benefit: the Sentry STORY-WEAVER-BACKEND-1 issue should now stop regenerating on every deploy. Verified on deploy `c7ff581d` 16:25:08 UTC — workers boot once, no restart, no audit_log error in logs. (closed by cb6a)

Took the SDK path instead: added a `beforeSend` hook in `lib/main.dart` that returns `null` for any event whose exception stack frames reference `dwds/src/injected/client.js`. Existing 33 events on STORY-WEAVER-5 already manually resolved in the Sentry UI; future occurrences will be dropped client-side before they leave the browser. (closed by cb6a) — Single recurring issue in the Sentry org for the past 2 months: `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'removeChild')` originating in `/dwds/src/injected/client.js` (Dart Web Debug Service — Flutter web dev-server tooling). 33 events, 0 users, every event tagged `environment:development`. Mechanism `auto.browser.global_handlers.onerror`, triggered when the dev page tears down and `dwds`' WebSocket-style channel calls `removeChild` on a node whose parent has already detached. Not a production bug. Two fixes (pick one): (a) **Sentry UI** — Settings → Inbound Filters → add rule "Filter events where environment matches development" (one-click, applies retroactively to future events); OR (b) **SDK code** — in `lib/main.dart`'s `SentryFlutter.init`, set `options.beforeSend` to drop events whose top frame matches `dwds/src/injected/client.js` AND `mechanism.type == 'auto.browser.global_handlers.onerror'`. Path (a) is preferred (no code change, also catches future dev-tooling noise). After applying, mark STORY-WEAVER-5 resolved in the Sentry UI; if it doesn't regress within 14 days the filter is working. Related: session df18's commit `fd730664` already sets `sampleRate=0` in non-release builds, which *should* have stopped this — but events keep arriving, so either Darcy's local `flutter run -d chrome` doesn't count as "non-release" under that gate, or the gate has a hole. - **MT-119** [done] (closed by 358e — picked option (c): updated `docs/agent-briefs/PLAYWRIGHT_SMOKE_TEST.md` with a new "COPPA parental consent — Playwright canonical approach" subsection documenting scroll-then-click against production; left the local-only `?bypass_consent=1` for dev use; deferred (a) HMAC bypass / (b) separate debug-build Railway service until continuous Playwright becomes a thing) MT-103 COPPA bypass is useless against production (created during MT-107 verify 2026-05-14) — MT-103 (commit `aab36482`) shipped a debug-only `?bypass_consent=1` URL param in `lib/screens/parental_consent_screen.dart` to skip the COPPA consent gate for Playwright smoke tests. Implementation gates the entire branch behind `kDebugMode` so it tree-shakes out of release builds. **The grand-light frontend on Railway is built in release mode** (`kDebugMode=false`), so the bypass never fires in production. MT-107 Playwright sweep confirmed: `?bypass_consent=1` had no effect against `https://grand-light-production-68d9.up.railway.app/`, fell back to manual scroll-then-accept. Three options: (a) **Remove the `kDebugMode` gate** and replace with a guard that requires a long secret URL param (e.g. `?bypass_consent=<HMAC of date+shared_secret>`) — bypass works in production but is unguessable; risk if the secret leaks. (b) **Stand up a separate Railway service** running a debug-mode Flutter web build at a non-public URL (most isolated, ~1-2 hr to wire); use this URL for Playwright smoke tests only. (c) **Accept the limitation** and document that Playwright sweeps against production must use the scroll-then-accept fallback (~20 sec per test, no infra cost). **Recommend (c) for now** — Playwright walks are infrequent enough that 20 sec/test is negligible, and the security surface of (a) or (b) isn't justified until smoke tests run continuously. After the superhero flow set `selectedScenario='superhero'`, the wizard popped back to Pick Place where any subsequent scene tap rewrote `selectedScenario` to that scene's id — silently demoting the request off the superhero dispatch path. **Fix:** changed both mapper gates to use the sturdier `heroPower != null && heroPower!.trim().isNotEmpty` signal — heroPower is set once in `superhero_power_screen.dart:163` and never touched by later wizard steps. MT-108's 350-word cap and MT-121's pinned villain roster both fully effective now. Tests: 4/4 new mapper tests in `test/screens/wizard_data_mapper_test.dart` pass. Debug-print rollback to follow.) MT-108 Explorer 350-word post-gen cap did NOT fire on production (created during MT-107 verify 2026-05-14) — MT-108 (commit `cb16f37f`, merged in `19f3b887`) shipped a 250-350 post-gen word cap for Explorer (age 6-8) Superhero stories at `backend/tasks/story_tasks.py:1032` (elif gating on `_sprout_age >= 6 and _sprout_age <= 8 and _is_superhero_theme(theme)`). MT-107 Playwright sweep on production immediately after deploy generated an Explorer Feeling Sense story (Maya, age 7) at **616 words** — almost 2× the cap. Either the elif gate never fired OR it fired but truncate failed silently. **Suspected root cause:** `lib/screens/wizard_steps/superhero_power_screen.dart:165` sets `wizardData.selectedScenario = 'superhero'` after the user picks a power, but `lib/screens/wizard_steps/hero_creator_scene_page.dart:175` resets `wizardData.selectedScenario = null;` and there are other writes to that field at lines 161, 620, 649. `lib/screens/wizard_steps/wizard_data_mapper.dart:97-99` only sets `theme = 'superhero'` when `data.selectedScenario == 'superhero'`. If any post-power-picker step clears or overwrites `selectedScenario`, the backend receives a non-superhero theme value and `_is_superhero_theme(theme)` returns false → the MT-108 elif never executes. The Playwright walk picked length="Story time" + style="Story Quest" AFTER the superhero power flow, which may have routed through one of the resetting steps. **Diagnostic plan:** (1) add a backend log line at the top of `generate_story_task` showing the received `theme` value alongside `age`, redeploy, re-run the Explorer Feeling Sense Playwright walk, and inspect Railway logs to confirm what theme value the backend actually saw; (2) add a Flutter `debugPrint` or assertion in `wizard_data_mapper.dart` confirming `selectedScenario` value at submission time. **Once root cause confirmed:** if a wizard step clears selectedScenario, fix that step to preserve it for superhero flows. Less likely alternative cause: `_truncate_to_word_cap` in `backend/tasks/story_tasks.py` returned None silently for the regen-then-truncate path; would need to inspect that helper if backend logs show the elif fired but story still landed at 616. ~30-60 min including diagnostic + fix + re-verify. - **MT-131** [done] Migrate Flux Schnell image generation from Replicate to Cloudflare Workers AI for genuinely-$0 cost at current scale (created 2026-05-15) — Per-page/cover illustrations currently route through Replicate Flux Schnell (`backend/replicate_image_generator.py` → `generate_story_illustration_flux_schnell`, `~$0.003/img`): it is the primary provider for ages 6+ and, as of this session, the last-resort fallback for Sprout (age <= 5) when Gemini-via-OpenRouter is empty. Cloudflare Workers AI exposes the **same model** (`@cf/black-forest-labs/flux-1-schnell`) under a free daily allocation (10,000 Neurons/day) that covers the app's entire current usage at **$0**. Darcy already has a Cloudflare account (used for the daily Postgres → R2 backup, see MT-122). **Scope:** (1) add a `CloudflareImageGenerator` (or a `generate_story_illustration_flux_cloudflare` method) calling `POST https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/accounts/<account_id>/ai/run/@cf/black-forest-labs/flux-1-schnell` with `{prompt, steps}`; response is raw image bytes (or base64 in newer API) — normalize to the existing `{id, prompt, image_data, format, generated_at}` dict shape so callers need no changes; (2) reuse the existing `_build_prompt()` for prompt parity (it already has the `age <= 5` warm-style modifier); (3) route the two Flux call sites in `backend/routes/story_routes.py` (age 6+ primary + the new Sprout fallback) to Cloudflare first, keep Replicate Flux as a secondary fallback so a Cloudflare outage/quota doesn't regress; (4) cost_tracker: log Cloudflare calls at `cost_usd=0` (or Neuron estimate). **New env vars needed (Darcy must create):** `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` and a scoped `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` with Workers AI read — create at dash.cloudflare.com → My Profile → API Tokens → Workers AI template; set in Railway backend service env + local `backend/.env`; add both to `backend/.env.example` (names only, no values). Add a `CLOUDFLARE_FLUX_DISABLED` kill-switch env mirroring `FLUX_SCHNELL_DISABLED`. **Verify:** generate a Sprout story with the OpenRouter/Gemini path forced empty (`DISABLE_GEMINI_IMAGE=true`) and confirm pictures still appear sourced from Cloudflare; generate an age-8 story and confirm Cloudflare is used (check `provider` tag / cost_tracker rows show `$0`). Update `backend/tests/unit/test_image_routing.py` for the new provider precedence. Outcome: per-page illustrations become free at current scale, Replicate cost drops to near-zero (fallback only). ~1.5–2 hr including the new generator + tests. **(closed by b95a — shipped 2026-05-16)** New `backend/cloudflare_image_generator.py` (`CloudflareImageGenerator.generate_story_illustration_flux` — normalizes the Workers AI base64-JPEG result to the shared `{id,prompt,image_data,format,generated_at}` dict, reuses Replicate `_build_prompt` for prompt parity, logs cost_tracker at `$0`); new `_generate_flux_illustration` helper in `story_routes.py` routes both Flux call sites (age 6+ primary + Sprout last-resort) Cloudflare-first → Replicate fallback; `CLOUDFLARE_FLUX_DISABLED` + `FLUX_SCHNELL_DISABLED` are independent kill-switches; `.env.example` documents the new vars; `test_image_routing.py` +7 tests, 28/28 green. Commit `f87eff3d`, deployed in `bc3c64b7`. `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` + `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` created (Workers AI–scoped token) and set on the Railway backend `story-weaver-app` service; deploy SUCCESS + healthy. Verified to "an age-8 story produces an image on every page"; Cloudflare-vs-Replicate provider NOT log-confirmed — Railway's log tooling did not surface the app's runtime `Cloudflare Flux …` log lines (deploy + http streams returned only startup lines). Darcy accepted as good-enough: Replicate fallback means zero regression whichever provider served. **Optional follow-up:** confirm Cloudflare is actually serving (curl POST to `/accounts/<id>/ai/run/@cf/black-forest-labs/flux-1-schnell` → expect `"success":true`, or query cost_tracker for `provider=cloudflare` rows). Local `backend/.env` was NOT given the Cloudflare vars — add them there too if you want to exercise the Cloudflare path in local dev.
- **MT-132** [done] Reader exit strands the user on the wizard review step after a freshly generated story (created 2026-05-15, found during MT-128 verify; fixed same day) — `_exitReader()` in `lib/story_result_screen.dart` did `Navigator.of(context).popUntil((route) => route.isFirst)` for a fresh story (`widget.storyId == null`). Because `lib/main_story.dart` `_AppEntryPoint.build()` renders `WizardStoryScreen` **as the root widget** for every onboarded user, the wizard *is* `route.isFirst`, so `popUntil(isFirst)` stranded the child on the wizard's "Make Magic" review step with a live "GO!" button. **Fix shipped:** `_exitReader` is now async — for a fresh story it loads `IsarService.getAllCharacters()` and `pushAndRemoveUntil`s a fresh `WizardStoryScreen(initialStep: 0, availableCharacters: …)`, landing the child on the welcome-back character grid. Verified via Playwright (exit fresh story → wizard "My Hero!" grid, not the GO! step; welcome-back grid with the saved hero confirmed). `flutter analyze` clean. (closed 2026-05-15) — **Follow-up surfaced → MT-133:** the b667 resume *entry points* (the "Continue your story" card AND the Continue/New bottom sheet) both live only on `StoryScreen` (`/story-home`), which the live flow never routes to; the wizard welcome-back grid that `_exitReader` now lands on has no continue affordance. See MT-133.
- **MT-133** [done] b667 resume/continue entry points are all on the orphaned `StoryScreen` — pick a home (created 2026-05-15, found during MT-132 fix; Option B implemented + verified 2026-05-16) — The b667 commit `f67c8981` touched only `lib/main_story.dart` + `lib/story_result_screen.dart`. Its two "resume" entry points — the "Continue your story" card and the `_showContinueOrNewSheet` Continue/Start-new bottom sheet (fired from `_StoryScreenState._onCharacterTapped`) — both live on `StoryScreen`, registered as the named route `/story-home` and commented "Keep old screen accessible". The live app never routes there: `_AppEntryPoint` renders `WizardStoryScreen` as root for every onboarded user. So although the resume *plumbing* works (page bookmark `story_progress_<id>` write/read + saved-story reopen-resumes-at-page — all verified 2026-05-15), the feature has **no reachable entry point**. After the MT-132 fix, exiting a story lands on the wizard welcome-back grid, where tapping a hero goes straight to the "Make Magic" review step — no Continue option. Also note: even on `StoryScreen` itself the Continue/New sheet didn't fire for a character that clearly had a recent story — `_recentStoryForCharacter` matches `character.name` against `story.characters[].name` and the match failed (saved-story `charactersJson` name mismatch — investigate). **Decision for Darcy — what is "home", and where do the resume affordances go:** (A) make `StoryScreen` the real post-onboarding home (`_AppEntryPoint` renders it instead of/above the wizard; wizard becomes a pushed flow) — resurrects the Continue card + sheet, but `StoryScreen` is flagged legacy and it's a bigger nav rework; OR (B) port a continue affordance onto the wizard's HeroCreatorStep welcome-back grid — e.g. a "Continue last story" button per hero card, or intercept the hero tap with the Continue/New sheet when `_recentStoryForCharacter` matches — keeps wizard-as-home (the Phase-5 direction) but touches wizard files b667 never did. Recommend (B). Either way, also fix the `_recentStoryForCharacter` name-match bug. Note: the standalone "Continue your story" card was removed from `StoryScreen` on 2026-05-15 (per Darcy's call that the per-hero sheet is the better primitive for multi-child households) — restore it only if (A) is chosen. ~1–3 hr depending on A vs B. **UPDATE 2026-05-16 — Option B implemented (Darcy's call), pending browser verify:** `lib/screens/wizard_steps/hero_creator_step.dart` now loads saved stories in `initState` (`_loadRecentStories`), exposes `_recentStoryForCharacter` (matches by character **id first, then name** — fixes the b667 name-only-match bug), and on the welcome-back grid (`_buildPage0`) any hero with a recent story (≤30 days) shows a "📖 Continue" badge and, on tap, opens a Continue / Start-a-new-story bottom sheet (`_showContinueOrNewSheet`) instead of jumping straight to "Make Magic". Continue → `_openSavedStory` (passes `storyId` so the reader resumes at the bookmark). `flutter analyze` clean (full project, 0 errors). Also: `_exitReader` in `story_result_screen.dart` now fetches characters from the backend `/get-characters` (Isar-snapshot fallback) before navigating — a hero created moments earlier in the same session hasn't synced to local Isar yet, so the local-only read returned empty and the wizard fell back to the create-a-hero form instead of the welcome-back grid. **Browser-verified 2026-05-16** via Playwright (Sprout age-3 walkthrough on a fresh `flutter run`): generated a story, exited the reader → wizard welcome-back grid showed the hero card with a "📖 Continue" badge → tapped it → Continue / Start-a-new-story sheet appeared → "Continue" reopened the saved story. `_recentStoryForCharacter` matched correctly. Changes uncommitted as of 2026-05-16 (`hero_creator_step.dart` + `story_result_screen.dart`).

- **MT-135** [open] Re-enable COPPA email verification before launch — flip `_kSkipEmailConsent` to `false` (created 2026-05-16, updated 2026-05-17) — During age-band UX testing the under-13 COPPA email round-trip ("Send Verification Email" → code entry) was made skippable. `lib/screens/parental_consent_screen.dart` has a top-level `const bool _kSkipEmailConsent = true;`; the `_requiresEmailVerification` getter (`_isUnder13 && !_kSkipEmailConsent`) gates the email path. ⚠️ **This flag now applies to ALL builds, release included** — it was previously `kDebugMode`-gated and release-safe; that is no longer true (changed 2026-05-17 so testers on the deployed build get the simple checkbox flow). While true, every build records `self_attested` consent (verified=false) directly via "Give Permission ✓" instead of the email round-trip, so the **currently-deployed tester build does NOT perform COPPA-verifiable consent**. Acceptable ONLY for a closed tester build with no real end users. Before launch: (1) set `_kSkipEmailConsent = false` to re-enable the email round-trip in production; (2) do a full under-13 consent walkthrough — enter parent email → receive code → enter code → child gains access — confirming `requestEmailVerification` / the `_ConsentPhase.awaitingCode` flow works end-to-end; (3) ensure `RESEND_API_KEY` and a verified sending domain (`CONSENT_EMAIL_FROM`, currently the `onboarding@resend.dev` test sender) are configured on Railway. Related: the separate `?bypass_consent=1` URL-param bypass (MT-103) is `kDebugMode`-only and can stay. ~10 min to flip + ~15 min verify.

- **MT-169** [done] `check_illustration_quota` fails OPEN on Redis outage — uncapped image-gen cost (created by MT-113 reopen 2026-05-19; closed 2026-05-19) — `backend/utils/ai_quota.py:664` returned `allowed=True` when Redis was unreachable, so a Redis outage uncapped image-gen cost for every free user. **Fixed** by adopting the M-2 / `check_daily_quota` shape: on Redis outage `check_illustration_quota` now falls back to the DB counter `User.illustrations_generated_this_month` enforced against `ILLUSTRATIONS_EMERGENCY_MULTIPLIER`-scaled cap (default 3× → free 30/month, sprout 180/month, family 600/month while Redis is down). `increment_illustration_quota` dual-writes to that DB column so it's a truthful baseline whenever Redis is healthy. `_maybe_reset_monthly` now rolls over both story- and illustration-counters on the same `usage_reset_date`. Double-outage (Redis down + DB unavailable) keeps the M-2 escape hatch — log an ERROR-level ALERT and allow, since hard-blocking on a double-outage takes the whole product down. Module docstring + `app_helpers.py` rate-limit comment updated to reflect the new fail-closed behaviour. Tests: 313/313 unit pass; 7 new tests in `test_image_routing.py` covering Redis-down + under-cap allow, at-cap block, env-multiplier override, Redis-GET-error fallback, Sprout cap, BYOK pass-through, and the no-DB escape hatch. Note: the rate-limit storage (`storage_uri=rate_limit_storage`) also uses Redis — that's separately handled by Flask-Limiter's in-memory fallback on outage, and the new hour/day expensive-tier limits from MT-113 act as defense-in-depth on top of this DB-backed monthly cap.

- **MT-181** [done — fixed by `2b347f3e` 2026-05-21; deploy verified live, Flutter web app renders again. Comment block in `nginx.conf` updated to document the dual script-src + connect-src requirement so the next CSP narrowing doesn't repeat this. New memory `flutter_web_csp_gstatic.md` records the gotcha. Pairs with the `web/index.html` brand-default fix shipped same day as `e58c9085`. (closed by 2026-05-21 start-session multi-agent run)] Production was blank for ~2 days — nginx CSP `connect-src` missing gstatic — discovered 2026-05-21 — Commit `f0edb28c` (2026-05-19 15:04, S-08 audit) narrowed `connect-src` from `'self' https: wss:` to two pinned origins (Railway backend + Sentry). Flutter Web's CanvasKit fetches `canvaskit.wasm` from `https://www.gstatic.com` via `fetch()` — script-src allowance only covers the `canvaskit.js` script load, not the wasm fetch. With gstatic absent from connect-src, the wasm 304/200 was blocked at the browser CSP layer, the engine never initialised, and every web client rendered blank from 2026-05-19 15:04 onward. Symptom was masked by the same-day Railway platform outage (MT-168 / MT-177) — when Railway recovered, the CSP regression was still deployed but the "blank page" got attributed to the outage and MT-177 closed assuming prod was healthy. Surfaced when chrome-devtools-mcp DevTools console was opened during MT-174 brand verification and revealed the CSP block. **Fix:** added `https://www.gstatic.com` to `connect-src` and updated the comment block to document that gstatic is required in **both** script-src (canvaskit.js load) AND connect-src (canvaskit.wasm fetch). **Lesson:** any future CSP tightening on a Flutter web deployment must keep gstatic in both directives. Captured in memory `flutter_web_csp_gstatic.md`.
- **MT-199** [open] Direction C "Pop-Up Picture Book" reader for Sprout — backlog, pending signal (created by MT-099 implementation 2026-05-29) — MT-099 shipped Direction B (Open Book) for all bands. Direction C (a cut-paper pop-up reader that drops `PageFlipBuilder` for an `AnimatedSwitcher`/`SlideTransition`/`RotationTransition` entrance, illustration-as-foreground + tilted text card, no two-page spread) was the unconventional option in `docs/MT-099-book-feel-design.md` §2 Direction C. **Decision:** do NOT build speculatively — it forks the reader into two code paths to maintain forever and is the highest a11y-risk surface (AnimatedSwitcher needs `Semantics(liveRegion: true)` per page or screen readers lose their place; the pop/tilt must honour reduce-motion — now available via `MotionPrefs`). **Scope it to Sprout (3-5) only**, NOT Explorer (6-8 readers may feel infantilised). **Trigger:** build it only once there's real signal that young readers find the flip metaphor confusing (user research / session observation). Until then this stays a backlog idea. Design + a11y notes already written in the design doc.
- **MT-200** [open] MT-099 optional polish: global warm "book-stage" background + title-page-leaf demotion (created by MT-099 implementation 2026-05-29) — Direction B shipped the leather `OpenBookFrame` which grounds the leaf, so the "floating card on purple void" complaint is addressed. Two higher-risk Direction-A/B items were deliberately de-scoped and are optional follow-ups if Darcy wants to push the book feel further: (1) **Global warm scaffold** — swap the per-band purple gradient behind the app bar + title + age-11+ `_buildReaderView` for a warm body tone. Risk: white app-bar chrome + white title text + the reader-layout text colours must be re-tinted and re-checked for ≥4.5:1 contrast across all 6 bands, and `_buildReaderView` must not regress — that's why it was held back. (2) **Title-page-leaf demotion** — move the title/gold-rule/reading-level pill off the purple area and onto a synthetic page-0 "title leaf" inside the book. Risk: inserting an index-0 leaf shifts every page index and interacts with `_hasCoverIllustration`, the `_storyPages` mapping, page labels, and the prefetcher's `textIndex` math — genuine surgery, needs its own test pass. Decision + design call for Darcy; neither is required for MT-099's core fix.
- **MT-303** [open] Adolescent antihero sensitivity-gate: copy + parental-involvement clarity (created by d66c, 2026-06-24) — The `ParentSensitivityInterstitial` shown before the Adolescent "double life" antihero flow (`lib/screens/wizard_steps/superhero_costume_screen.dart:299`, questTitle "Live a double life", topics identity & secrets / feeling not okay / being seen) is a content **heads-up, NOT a consent mechanism** — and correctly so, since 15-17 need no COPPA parental consent. Two open questions to settle **before turning the teen band on** (non-blocking for the kids'-band soft launch, which keeps Adolescent OFF): (1) **Copy** is addressed "A QUIET HEADS-UP FOR A GROWN-UP" with a "Start the story" button, but a solo 15-17 user reads it themselves — consider neutral wording that lands for either a teen or a co-present parent. (2) **Design decision:** does the "That I'm not okay" distress mechanic need *actual* parental involvement, or is awareness-only the right call? The current widget is awareness + a soft off-ramp + crisis resources (inline #305 and story-end #309); it does NOT guarantee a parent is present (the app can't know). Ties to [[MT-266c]] (human clinical sign-off — the real launch gate for this band) and MT-294 (distress-mechanic decision). ~decision + minor copy edit.
